queries\u003c/P\u003e\n\u003c/TD\u003e\n\u003cTD class=\"lia-align-right\"\u003e\n\u003cP\u003e38.1\u003c/P\u003e\n\u003c/TD\u003e\n\u003cTD class=\"lia-align-right\"\u003e\n\u003cP\u003e44.7\u003c/P\u003e\n\u003c/TD\u003e\n\u003cTD class=\"lia-align-right\"\u003e\n\u003cP\u003e46.7\u003c/P\u003e\n\u003c/TD\u003e\n\u003cTD class=\"lia-align-right\"\u003e\n\u003cP\u003e\u003cSTRONG\u003e59.9\u003c/STRONG\u003e\u003c/P\u003e\n\u003c/TD\u003e\n\u003c/TR\u003e\n\u003cTR\u003e\n\u003cTD\u003e\n\u003cP\u003eShort queries\u003c/P\u003e\n\u003c/TD\u003e\n\u003cTD class=\"lia-align-right\"\u003e\n\u003cP\u003e53.1\u003c/P\u003e\n\u003c/TD\u003e\n\u003cTD class=\"lia-align-right\"\u003e\n\u003cP\u003e38.8\u003c/P\u003e\n\u003c/TD\u003e\n\u003cTD class=\"lia-align-right\"\u003e\n\u003cP\u003e53.0\u003c/P\u003e\n\u003c/TD\u003e\n\u003cTD class=\"lia-align-right\"\u003e\n\u003cP\u003e\u003cSTRONG\u003e63.9\u003c/STRONG\u003e\u003c/P\u003e\n\u003c/TD\u003e\n\u003c/TR\u003e\n\u003c/TBODY\u003e\n\u003c/TABLE\u003e\n\u003c/DIV\u003e\n\u003cP\u003e\u003cEM\u003e\u003cSTRONG\u003eTable 2\u003c/STRONG\u003e: NDCG@3 comparison across query types and search configurations. See \u003cA href=\"#querytype\" target=\"_self\" rel=\"noopener\"\u003e§6.3 Query Type definitions for Table 2\u003c/A\u003e for a more detailed description of each query type. All vector retrieval modes used the same document chunks (512 token chunks w/25% overlap with Ada-002 embedding model over customer query/document benchmark). Sentence boundaries were preserved in all cases.\u003c/EM\u003e\u003c/P\u003e\n\u003cP\u003e\u0026nbsp;\u003c/P\u003e\n\u003cH2\u003e4. Your Document Chunking strategy matters\u003c/H2\u003e\n\u003cP\u003eChunking solves 3 problems for Generative AI applications:\u0026nbsp;\u003c/P\u003e\n\u003cOL\u003e\n\u003cLI\u003eSplitting long documents into limited-length passages allows multiple retrieved documents to be passed to the LLM within its context window limit.\u003c/LI\u003e\n\u003cLI\u003eChunking provides a mechanism for the most relevant passages of a given document to be ranked first.\u003c/LI\u003e\n\u003cLI\u003eVector search has a per-model limit to how much content can be embedded into each vector.\u003c/LI\u003e\n\u003c/OL\u003e\n\u003cP\u003e\u0026nbsp;\u003c/P\u003e\n\u003cP\u003eEmbedding each chunk into its own vector keeps the input within the embedding model’s token limit and enables the entire document to be searchable in an ANN search index without truncation. Most deep embedding models have a limit of 512 tokens. Ada-002 has a limit of 8,192 tokens. Moderate length documents can have tens of thousands of tokens. The benefit of chunking is particularly strong when the documents are very long or the answers to queries are found later in the document:\u003c/P\u003e\n\u003cP\u003e\u0026nbsp;\u003c/P\u003e\n\u003cDIV class=\"styles_lia-table-wrapper__h6Xo9 styles_table-responsive__MW0lN\"\u003e\n\u003cTABLE class=\"lia-indent-margin-left-30px\" border=\"1\"\u003e\n\u003cTBODY\u003e\n\u003cTR\u003e\n\u003cTD\u003e\n\u003cP\u003e\u003cSTRONG\u003eRetrieval Configuration\u003c/STRONG\u003e\u003c/P\u003e\n\u003cP\u003e\u0026nbsp;\u003c/P\u003e\n\u003cP\u003e\u0026nbsp;\u003c/P\u003e\n\u003c/TD\u003e\n\u003cTD class=\"lia-align-right\"\u003e\n\u003cP\u003e\u003cSTRONG\u003eSingle vector per document\u003c/STRONG\u003e\u003c/P\u003e\n\u003cP\u003e\u003cEM\u003e\u0026nbsp;[Recall@50]\u003c/EM\u003e\u003c/P\u003e\n\u003c/TD\u003e\n\u003cTD class=\"lia-align-right\"\u003e\n\u003cP\u003e\u003cSTRONG\u003eChunked documents\u003c/STRONG\u003e\u003c/P\u003e\n\u003cP\u003e\u0026nbsp;\u003cEM\u003e[Recall@50]\u003c/EM\u003e\u003c/P\u003e\n\u003c/TD\u003e\n\u003c/TR\u003e\n\u003cTR\u003e\n\u003cTD\u003e\n\u003cP\u003eQueries whose answer is in long documents\u003c/P\u003e\n\u003c/TD\u003e\n\u003cTD class=\"lia-align-right\"\u003e\n\u003cP\u003e28.2\u003c/P\u003e\n\u003c/TD\u003e\n\u003cTD class=\"lia-align-right\"\u003e\n\u003cP\u003e\u003cSTRONG\u003e45.7 \u003c/STRONG\u003e\u003c/P\u003e\n\u003c/TD\u003e\n\u003c/TR\u003e\n\u003cTR\u003e\n\u003cTD\u003e\n\u003cP\u003eQueries whose answer is deep into a document\u003c/P\u003e\n\u003c/TD\u003e\n\u003cTD class=\"lia-align-right\"\u003e\n\u003cP\u003e28.7\u003c/P\u003e\n\u003c/TD\u003e\n\u003cTD class=\"lia-align-right\"\u003e\n\u003cP\u003e\u003cSTRONG\u003e51.4 \u003c/STRONG\u003e\u003c/P\u003e\n\u003c/TD\u003e\n\u003c/TR\u003e\n\u003c/TBODY\u003e\n\u003c/TABLE\u003e\n\u003c/DIV\u003e\n\u003cP\u003e\u003cEM\u003e\u003cSTRONG\u003eTable 3\u003c/STRONG\u003e: Recall@50 comparison using (1) a single vector to represent each document (first 4096 tokens of each document were vectorized and the rest were truncated) vs (2) chunking each document into 512 token chunks w/25% overlap with Ada-002 embedding model over customer query/document benchmark. Sentence boundaries were preserved in all cases. Metric computed with vector retrieval only (no Semantic ranking).\u0026nbsp;\u003c/EM\u003e\u003c/P\u003e\n\u003cP\u003e\u0026nbsp;\u003c/P\u003e\n\u003cP\u003eAnother key consideration is that embedding models must compress all the semantic content of a passage into a limited number of floating-point numbers (e.g. Ada-002 uses 1,536 dimensions). If developers encode a long passage with multiple topics into a single vector, important nuance can get lost. Our analysis shows that using large chunks reduces retrieval performance.\u003c/P\u003e\n\u003cP\u003e\u0026nbsp;\u003c/P\u003e\n\u003cDIV class=\"styles_lia-table-wrapper__h6Xo9 styles_table-responsive__MW0lN\"\u003e\n\u003cTABLE class=\"lia-indent-margin-left-30px\" border=\"1\"\u003e\n\u003cTBODY\u003e\n\u003cTR\u003e\n\u003cTD\u003e\n\u003cP\u003e\u003cSTRONG\u003eRetrieval Configuration \u003c/STRONG\u003e\u003c/P\u003e\n\u003c/TD\u003e\n\u003cTD class=\"lia-align-right\"\u003e\n\u003cP\u003e\u003cSTRONG\u003eRecall@50\u003c/STRONG\u003e\u003c/P\u003e\n\u003c/TD\u003e\n\u003c/TR\u003e\n\u003cTR\u003e\n\u003cTD\u003e\n\u003cP\u003e512 input tokens per vector\u003c/P\u003e\n\u003c/TD\u003e\n\u003cTD class=\"lia-align-right\"\u003e\n\u003cP\u003e\u003cSTRONG\u003e42.4\u003c/STRONG\u003e\u003c/P\u003e\n\u003c/TD\u003e\n\u003c/TR\u003e\n\u003cTR\u003e\n\u003cTD\u003e\n\u003cP\u003e1024 input tokens per vector\u003c/P\u003e\n\u003c/TD\u003e\n\u003cTD class=\"lia-align-right\"\u003e\n\u003cP\u003e37.5\u003c/P\u003e\n\u003c/TD\u003e\n\u003c/TR\u003e\n\u003cTR\u003e\n\u003cTD\u003e\n\u003cP\u003e4096 input tokens per vector\u003c/P\u003e\n\u003c/TD\u003e\n\u003cTD class=\"lia-align-right\"\u003e\n\u003cP\u003e36.4\u003c/P\u003e\n\u003c/TD\u003e\n\u003c/TR\u003e\n\u003cTR\u003e\n\u003cTD\u003e\n\u003cP\u003e8191 input tokens per vector\u003c/P\u003e\n\u003c/TD\u003e\n\u003cTD class=\"lia-align-right\"\u003e\n\u003cP\u003e34.9\u003c/P\u003e\n\u003c/TD\u003e\n\u003c/TR\u003e\n\u003c/TBODY\u003e\n\u003c/TABLE\u003e\n\u003c/DIV\u003e\n\u003cP\u003e\u003cEM\u003e\u003cSTRONG\u003eTable 4\u003c/STRONG\u003e: Recall@50 comparison of different chunk sizes with Ada-002 embedding model over customer query/document benchmark. Sentence boundaries were preserved in all cases. Metric computed with vector retrieval only (no Semantic ranking).\u003c/EM\u003e\u003c/P\u003e\n\u003cP\u003e\u0026nbsp;\u003c/P\u003e\n\u003cP\u003eThere are many ways developers can build the input for each vector. For example, they can overlap each chunk so there is shared context between them, or they can add in the document title or key topics into each vector to give more context. A strategy of terminating vectors at natural sentence and paragraph breaks is both simple and effective.\u0026nbsp;\u003c/P\u003e\n\u003cP\u003e\u0026nbsp;\u003c/P\u003e\n\u003cDIV class=\"styles_lia-table-wrapper__h6Xo9 styles_table-responsive__MW0lN\"\u003e\n\u003cTABLE class=\"lia-indent-margin-left-30px\" border=\"1\"\u003e\n\u003cTBODY\u003e\n\u003cTR\u003e\n\u003cTD\u003e\n\u003cP\u003e\u003cSTRONG\u003eChunk boundary strategy\u003c/STRONG\u003e\u003c/P\u003e\n\u003c/TD\u003e\n\u003cTD class=\"lia-align-right\"\u003e\n\u003cP\u003e\u003cSTRONG\u003eRecall@50\u003c/STRONG\u003e\u003c/P\u003e\n\u003c/TD\u003e\n\u003c/TR\u003e\n\u003cTR\u003e\n\u003cTD\u003e\n\u003cP\u003e512 tokens, break at token boundary\u003c/P\u003e\n\u003c/TD\u003e\n\u003cTD class=\"lia-align-right\"\u003e\n\u003cP\u003e40.9\u003c/P\u003e\n\u003c/TD\u003e\n\u003c/TR\u003e\n\u003cTR\u003e\n\u003cTD\u003e\n\u003cP\u003e512 tokens, preserve sentence boundaries\u003c/P\u003e\n\u003c/TD\u003e\n\u003cTD class=\"lia-align-right\"\u003e\n\u003cP\u003e42.4\u003c/P\u003e\n\u003c/TD\u003e\n\u003c/TR\u003e\n\u003cTR\u003e\n\u003cTD\u003e\n\u003cP\u003e512 tokens with 10% overlapping chunks\u003c/P\u003e\n\u003c/TD\u003e\n\u003cTD class=\"lia-align-right\"\u003e\n\u003cP\u003e43.1\u003c/P\u003e\n\u003c/TD\u003e\n\u003c/TR\u003e\n\u003cTR\u003e\n\u003cTD\u003e\n\u003cP\u003e512 tokens with 25% overlapping chunks\u003c/P\u003e\n\u003c/TD\u003e\n\u003cTD class=\"lia-align-right\"\u003e\n\u003cP\u003e\u003cSTRONG\u003e43.9\u003c/STRONG\u003e\u003c/P\u003e\n\u003c/TD\u003e\n\u003c/TR\u003e\n\u003c/TBODY\u003e\n\u003c/TABLE\u003e\n\u003c/DIV\u003e\n\u003cP\u003e\u003cEM\u003e\u003cSTRONG\u003eTable 5\u003c/STRONG\u003e: Recall@50 comparison of different chunk boundary strategies using 512 tokens with Ada002 embedding model over customer query/document benchmark. Metric computed with vector retrieval only (no semantic ranking).\u003c/EM\u003e\u003c/P\u003e\n\u003cP\u003e\u0026nbsp;\u003c/P\u003e\n\u003cH2\u003e5. Semantic Ranking Puts the Best Results at the Top\u003c/H2\u003e\n\u003cP\u003eGenerative AI scenarios typically use the top 3 to 5 results as their grounding context to prioritize the most important results. AI Search applications work best with a calibrated relevance score that can be used to filter out low quality results.\u0026nbsp;\u003c/P\u003e\n\u003cP\u003e\u0026nbsp;\u003c/P\u003e\n\u003cP\u003eThe semantic ranker runs the query and documents text simultaneously though transformer models that utilize the cross-attention mechanism to produce a ranker score. The query and document chunk score is calibrated to a range that is consistent across all indexes and queries. A score of 0 represents a very irrelevant chunk, and a score of 4 represents an excellent one. In the chart below, Hybrid + Semantic ranking finds the best content for the LLM at each result set size.\u0026nbsp;\u003c/P\u003e\n\u003cLI-IMAGE id=\"kZyUwK\" caption=\"false\" alt=\"\" align=\"center\" resized=\"false\" size=\"default\" layout=\"stretch\" name=\"\" src=\"https://techcommunity.microsoft.com/t5/s/gxcuf89792/images/bS0zOTI5MTY3LWtaeVV3Sw?image-dimensions=750x750\u0026amp;revision=43\"\u003e\u003c/LI-IMAGE\u003e\n\u003cP\u003e\u003cEM\u003e\u003cSTRONG\u003eChart 1\u003c/STRONG\u003e: Percentage of queries where high-quality chunks are found in the top 1 to 5 results, compared across search configurations. All retrieval modes used the same set of customer query/document benchmark. Document chunks were 512 tokens with 25% overlap.\u0026nbsp; Vector and hybrid retrieval used Ada-002 embeddings.\u0026nbsp;\u003c/EM\u003e\u003c/P\u003e\n\u003cP\u003e\u0026nbsp;\u003c/P\u003e\n\u003cP\u003eIn conclusion, the results of the above experiments on real-world and benchmark datasets lead us to recommend the combined strategies of chunked content, hybrid search, and semantic ranking. To test these findings against your users’ questions and datasets, please try the resources linked below to get started:\u003c/P\u003e\n\u003cUL\u003e\n\u003cLI\u003e\u003cA href=\"https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/search/\" target=\"_self\"\u003eAzure AI Search documentation | Microsoft Learn\u003c/A\u003e\u003c/LI\u003e\n\u003cLI\u003e\u003cA href=\"https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/search/semantic-search-overview\" target=\"_self\"\u003eSemantic ranker (formerly semantic search) - Azure AI Search | Microsoft Learn\u003c/A\u003e\u003c/LI\u003e\n\u003c/UL\u003e\n\u003cP\u003e\u0026nbsp;\u003c/P\u003e\n\u003cH2\u003e6. Appendix – How we ran these tests\u003c/H2\u003e\n\u003cP\u003e\u0026nbsp;\u003c/P\u003e\n\u003cH4 id=\"generated\"\u003e6.1 How we generated the numbers in this post\u003c/H4\u003e\n\u003cP\u003eTo assess which retrieval systems and configurations performed the best, we followed best practices to generate comparable metrics. The high-level process was to replay a list of queries against several document indexes for each configuration and produce scores of how good the retrieval and ranking was.\u0026nbsp;\u003c/P\u003e\n\u003cUL\u003e\n\u003cLI\u003e\u003cSTRONG\u003eDocuments\u003c/STRONG\u003e - We use a consistent set of documents sourced from either Azure customers (with their permission) or publicly available benchmarks.\u0026nbsp;\u003c/LI\u003e\n\u003cLI\u003e\u003cSTRONG\u003eQueries\u003c/STRONG\u003e – We used a combination of end user queries and/or queries generated by several different GPT prompts using random snippets from the document index as grounding.\u003c/LI\u003e\n\u003cLI\u003e\u003cSTRONG\u003eScoring\u003c/STRONG\u003e – We used benchmark-provided labels (and the official scoring library) for BEIR and other datasets. For customer datasets, we use a GPT prompt that was vetted against a library of high quality (internally reviewed) human ground-truth labels.\u003c/LI\u003e\n\u003c/UL\u003e\n\u003cP\u003e\u0026nbsp;\u003c/P\u003e\n\u003cP\u003eWe used 3 metrics to determine our recommendations:\u003c/P\u003e\n\u003cUL\u003e\n\u003cLI\u003e\u003cSTRONG\u003eNDCG@10\u003c/STRONG\u003e – NDCG is a common information retrieval metric that provides a score between 0 and 100 based on how well a retrieval system (1) found the best results and (2) put those results in the ideal order (i.e. a sorted list from the best document to the worst) for all the queries in a given query set. The @10 means that the top 10 documents were considered in the score calculation. We used this metric and the pool of available labels for public benchmarks to be consistent with previous runs. \u003cA href=\"https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Discounted_cumulative_gain#Normalized_DCG\" target=\"_self\"\u003eNormalized Discounted Cumulative Gain (NDCG)\u003c/A\u003e\u0026nbsp;\u003c/LI\u003e\n\u003cLI\u003e\u003cSTRONG\u003eNDCG@3\u003c/STRONG\u003e – The same NDCG metric but computed on the top 3 documents. We use @3 because we aim to get the most accurate results in the top (3) for generative AI scenarios. We score the top 50 documents because Azure AI Search’s semantic ranker works on the top 50 results.\u003c/LI\u003e\n\u003cLI\u003e\u003cSTRONG\u003eRecall@50\u003c/STRONG\u003e – We count the number of documents that our scoring prompt rates as high quality within the top 50 retrieved results and divide it by the number of known good documents for that query.\u003c/LI\u003e\n\u003c/UL\u003e\n\u003cH4 id=\"searchconfiguration\"\u003e6.2 Search and Dataset configuration for Table 1\u003c/H4\u003e\n\u003cP\u003e\u003cSTRONG\u003eSearch Configuration\u003c/STRONG\u003e\u003c/P\u003e\n\u003cP\u003eFor this table of results, all documents were broken into 512 token chunks w/25% overlap.\u003c/P\u003e\n\u003cUL\u003e\n\u003cLI\u003e\u003cSTRONG\u003eKeyword\u003c/STRONG\u003e: The full set of chunks were indexed as if each chunk was a full document. Searches were performed as usual with the keyword-based index (BM25 similarity) and we labeled the top 50.\u003c/LI\u003e\n\u003cLI\u003e\u003cSTRONG\u003eVector\u003c/STRONG\u003e: All the chunks were embedded using Ada-002 and an ANN index was built. Each query was also embedded with Ada-002 and searched using cosine similarity. The top 50 were labeled.\u003c/LI\u003e\n\u003cLI\u003e\u003cSTRONG\u003eHybrid\u003c/STRONG\u003e: The keyword index and vector index of the chunks were searched (taking the top 50 from each) and then the results were fused together using RRF. The top 50 documents (chunks) from RRF were labeled.\u003c/LI\u003e\n\u003cLI\u003e\u003cSTRONG\u003eHybrid + \u003cA href=\"https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/search/semantic-search-overview\" target=\"_self\"\u003esemantic ranking\u003c/A\u003e\u003c/STRONG\u003e: Queries were performed against the hybrid search configuration with semantic ranking enabled.\u003c/LI\u003e\n\u003c/UL\u003e\n\u003cP\u003e\u0026nbsp;\u003c/P\u003e\n\u003cP\u003e\u003cSTRONG\u003eDataset details\u003c/STRONG\u003e\u003c/P\u003e\n\u003cUL\u003e\n\u003cLI\u003e\u003cSTRONG\u003eCustomer datasets\u003c/STRONG\u003e – retrieval benchmarks built from 4 different customer datasets spanned industries and document structures. All documents were imported from raw inputs (e.g. pptx, pdf, html) using Azure AI Search’s document ingestion pipeline. Queries for each dataset are a mixture of provided and GPT-generated queries. For ANN vector retrieval tests, all documents were chunked into 512 tokens with 25% overlap and embedded using Ada-002.\u003c/LI\u003e\n\u003cLI\u003e\u003cSTRONG\u003eBEIR\u003c/STRONG\u003e – Imported the following datasets from the official source (\u003cA href=\"https://github.com/beir-cellar/beir\" target=\"_self\"\u003ebeir-cellar/beir: A Heterogeneous Benchmark for Information Retrieval. Easy to use, evaluate your models across 15+ diverse IR datasets. (github.com)\u003c/A\u003e) and used the official labels and official scoring library to generate metrics. For ANN vector retrieval, each document was indexed using Ada-002 (in 99.9% of cases 1 vector was sufficient to represent the full document). Beir datasets used: nq, scidocs, touche2020, arguana, climate-fever, dbpedia, fever, hotpotqa, covid.\u0026nbsp;\u003c/LI\u003e\n\u003cLI\u003e\u003cSTRONG\u003eMiracl\u003c/STRONG\u003e – Imported directly from the official source (\u003cA href=\"https://github.com/project-miracl/miracl\" target=\"_self\"\u003eproject-miracl/miracl: A large-scale multilingual dataset for Information Retrieval. Thorough human-annotations across 18 diverse languages. (github.com)\u003c/A\u003e) and used official labels for scoring. Due to the size of the dataset the following simplifications were used: we sampled 250 queries per language and only documents with labels were vectorized and a combined multi-lingual index was built. The reported metric is the average across ar, bn, de, en, es, fa, fi, fr, hi, id, ja, ko, ru, sw, te, th, yo, zh languages.\u003c/LI\u003e\n\u003c/UL\u003e\n\u003cP\u003e\u0026nbsp;\u003c/P\u003e\n\u003cH4 id=\"querytype\"\u003e6.3 Query Type definitions for Table 2\u003c/H4\u003e\n\u003cDIV class=\"styles_lia-table-wrapper__h6Xo9 styles_table-responsive__MW0lN\"\u003e\n\u003cTABLE border=\"1\"\u003e\n\u003cTBODY\u003e\n\u003cTR\u003e\n\u003cTD\u003e\n\u003cP\u003e\u003cSTRONG\u003eQuery type\u003c/STRONG\u003e\u003c/P\u003e\n\u003c/TD\u003e\n\u003cTD\u003e\n\u003cP\u003e\u003cSTRONG\u003eExplanation\u003c/STRONG\u003e\u003c/P\u003e\n\u003c/TD\u003e\n\u003cTD\u003e\n\u003cP\u003e\u003cSTRONG\u003eExample\u003c/STRONG\u003e\u003c/P\u003e\n\u003c/TD\u003e\n\u003c/TR\u003e\n\u003cTR\u003e\n\u003cTD\u003e\n\u003cP\u003eConcept seeking queries\u003c/P\u003e\n\u003c/TD\u003e\n\u003cTD\u003e\n\u003cP\u003eAbstract questions that require multiple sentences to answer\u003c/P\u003e\n\u003c/TD\u003e\n\u003cTD\u003e\n\u003cP\u003e“Why should I use semantic search to rank results?”\u003c/P\u003e\n\u003c/TD\u003e\n\u003c/TR\u003e\n\u003cTR\u003e\n\u003cTD\u003e\n\u003cP\u003eExact snippet search\u003c/P\u003e\n\u003c/TD\u003e\n\u003cTD\u003e\n\u003cP\u003eLonger queries that are exact sub-strings from the original paragraph\u003c/P\u003e\n\u003c/TD\u003e\n\u003cTD\u003e\n\u003cP\u003e“enables you to maximize the quality and value of your LLM investments most efficiently by feeding only relevant information”\u003c/P\u003e\n\u003c/TD\u003e\n\u003c/TR\u003e\n\u003cTR\u003e\n\u003cTD\u003e\n\u003cP\u003eWeb search-like queries\u003c/P\u003e\n\u003c/TD\u003e\n\u003cTD\u003e\n\u003cP\u003eShortened queries similar to those commonly entered into a search engine\u003c/P\u003e\n\u003c/TD\u003e\n\u003cTD\u003e\n\u003cP\u003e“Best retrieval concept queries”\u003c/P\u003e\n\u003c/TD\u003e\n\u003c/TR\u003e\n\u003cTR\u003e\n\u003cTD\u003e\n\u003cP\u003eLow query/doc term overlap\u003c/P\u003e\n\u003c/TD\u003e\n\u003cTD\u003e\n\u003cP\u003eQueries where the answer uses different words and phrases from the question [which can be challenging for a retrieval engine to find]\u003c/P\u003e\n\u003c/TD\u003e\n\u003cTD\u003e\n\u003cP\u003e“greatest technology for sorting” searching for a document that says: “Azure AI Search has the best models for ranking your content”\u003c/P\u003e\n\u003c/TD\u003e\n\u003c/TR\u003e\n\u003cTR\u003e\n\u003cTD\u003e\n\u003cP\u003eFact seeking queries\u003c/P\u003e\n\u003c/TD\u003e\n\u003cTD\u003e\n\u003cP\u003eQueries with a single, clear answer\u003c/P\u003e\n\u003c/TD\u003e\n\u003cTD\u003e\n\u003cP\u003e“How many documents are semantically ranked”\u003c/P\u003e\n\u003c/TD\u003e\n\u003c/TR\u003e\n\u003cTR\u003e\n\u003cTD\u003e\n\u003cP\u003eKeyword queries\u003c/P\u003e\n\u003c/TD\u003e\n\u003cTD\u003e\n\u003cP\u003eShort queries that consist of only the important identifier words.\u003c/P\u003e\n\u003c/TD\u003e\n\u003cTD\u003e\n\u003cP\u003e“semantic ranker”\u003c/P\u003e\n\u003c/TD\u003e\n\u003c/TR\u003e\n\u003cTR\u003e\n\u003cTD\u003e\n\u003cP\u003eQueries with misspellings\u003c/P\u003e\n\u003c/TD\u003e\n\u003cTD\u003e\n\u003cP\u003eQueries with typos, transpositions and common misspellings introduced\u003c/P\u003e\n\u003c/TD\u003e\n\u003cTD\u003e\n\u003cP\u003e“Ho w mny documents are samantically r4nked”\u003c/P\u003e\n\u003c/TD\u003e\n\u003c/TR\u003e\n\u003cTR\u003e\n\u003cTD\u003e\n\u003cP\u003eLong queries\u003c/P\u003e\n\u003c/TD\u003e\n\u003cTD\u003e\n\u003cP\u003eQueries longer than 20 tokens\u003c/P\u003e\n\u003c/TD\u003e\n\u003cTD\u003e\n\u003cP\u003e“This is a very long query that uses a lot of tokens in its composition and structure because it is verbose”\u003c/P\u003e\n\u003c/TD\u003e\n\u003c/TR\u003e\n\u003cTR\u003e\n\u003cTD\u003e\n\u003cP\u003eMedium queries\u003c/P\u003e\n\u003c/TD\u003e\n\u003cTD\u003e\n\u003cP\u003eBetween 5 and 20 tokens long\u003c/P\u003e\n\u003c/TD\u003e\n\u003cTD\u003e\n\u003cP\u003e“This is a medium length query”\u003c/P\u003e\n\u003c/TD\u003e\n\u003c/TR\u003e\n\u003cTR\u003e\n\u003cTD\u003e\n\u003cP\u003eShort queries\u003c/P\u003e\n\u003c/TD\u003e\n\u003cTD\u003e\n\u003cP\u003eQueries shorter than 5 tokens\u003c/P\u003e\n\u003c/TD\u003e\n\u003cTD\u003e\n\u003cP\u003e“Short
